ParlaMint-SI_2016-03-04-SDZ7-Redna-16.ana.u1	Dear fellow Members and fellow Members, ladies and gentlemen! I begin with the continuation of the 16th session of the National Assembly. I am informed that today's meeting cannot be attended by the following Members: Mrs Eva Irgl, Mrs Suzanne Lepi Šimenko, Mrs Irena Grošelj Kosnik, Mrs Iva Dimic, Mrs Jelka Godec, Mrs Marija Bačič, Mr Marijan Pojbič, Mr Matjaž Hanžek, mag. Matthew Tonin, Mr Jernej Vrtovec, Luka Mesec, Miha Kordiš, Mr Franz Breznik and Mr Peter Wilphan. I say hello to everyone who's here! We're moving to 8. This is a proposal for a Declaration on guidelines for the operations of the Republic of Slovenia in the institutions of the European Union between January 2016 and June 2017. The proposal for a declaration was submitted by the Government for consideration by the assembly. I give the floor to Dr Mir Cerar, President of the Government of the Republic of Slovenia, to further explain the proposal for a declaration.
ParlaMint-SI_2016-03-04-SDZ7-Redna-16.ana.u2	Honourable President of the National Assembly, honourable Members and Members of Parliament! Europe may have been at a crossroads more than ever. In the midst of the economic and migration crisis, the situation in the immediate neighbourhood and the crisis of running on the old continent, there are increasing differences. In the absence of effective common solutions, the place of uncertainty, egoism and harmful populism is widening with the passiveness of countries not directly involved in the current crisis. That reminds us dangerously of some other times, times we certainly don't want. It is for this reason that the fundamental values on the basis of which European integration has emerged need to be more aware. I am primarily talking about peace, security, solidarity and a sense of fellow man. If ever, now is the time for unity to work together to find the right balance between humanity and security, saving and the social state, between peaceful policy and the demands of the international environment, to participate in crisis resolution missions. It is time for common sense and a constructive search for solutions, both at home and in Europe and throughout the world. Your Honor! In such an environment, it is, of course, difficult to plan for one year and a half in full confidence, but under the Cooperation Act between the Government and the National Assembly in European Union matters, the Government has the task of preparing guidelines for the functioning of the State in the institutions of the European Union every 18 months. We must take into account the broader framework that the European Commission's work programme and the Tria Presidency programme of the Council of the EU are linked to each time. This declaration has been the tenth in line since Slovenia is a member of the European Union. The proposal for a declaration, which has already been discussed in more detail in the home work bodies, sets out the key priorities and guidelines that Slovenia will follow in its work in the EU institutions. Allow me to briefly describe some of the key topics that will undoubtedly mark the European Union and the position of Slovenia in the next year and a half. First of all, it is imperative to highlight the most pressing topic, which is, of course, far ahead of the work of both Slovenia and the European Union, that is, the migration crisis. Slovenia has always advocated comprehensive European solutions based on the spirit of cooperation and solidarity. Even at a time when the latter had to be cut off after less popular short-term responses, we did so. We are recognised by many at international level by the principle, consistency and constructiveness of our efforts, so we are once again active in the international field. Unfortunately, everyone does not respect enough European values and commitments. Some in the public still advocate one thing, but they practice something completely different. This has to be done. Migrants must be clearly told at the beginning of the dangerous journey what they can expect at the end. In our search for solutions, we are primarily working towards common responses that strengthen the efforts of the European Union to achieve its achievements. It is high time that we immediately establish the normal functioning of the Schengen area, return to the full exercise of the control of the Schengen borders and thus remove the reasons for any extension of internal border control. This will also be my key message at the leaders' meeting next week. At the same time, care must be taken to ensure that there is no possible humanitarian disaster or friction in the Balkans. That is why we have been actively working for a long time on closer cooperation between the Western Balkan countries, both at political and operational level. And in doing so, we constantly stress the necessity of aid to Greece and Macedonia, which are most at risk. Above all, it is also necessary to help those who, of course, need help. The flow of irregular migration must be halted and replaced by regular migration, so that they can be more humane. Indeed, the agreed transfers of those eligible for protection and the consistent return of those not should be initiated. In the long-term resolution of the migration crisis, the active role of Turkey is crucial. It is true that Turkey is already very burdened by the migration crisis itself, but it is also true that it can do much more in its resolution. We therefore need other, complementary or complementary solutions to address the migration crisis. It is still crucial that diplomatic efforts continue to address the causes of the migration crisis, which will also be one of the important objectives of the European Union's Common Foreign and Security Policy in the next 18 months. The end of the conflict in Syria, the military and ideological defeat of DAESH and ensuring stability and security in the Middle East and North Africa are key objectives. But, of course, that's not enough. The resolution of the migration crisis must take into account the fact that many people are leaving their homes also due to poor economic conditions, poverty, human rights violations and ever-increasing climate change. To address these challenges, much more attention should therefore be paid in the future. The fight against smugglers and illegal trafficking in human beings will take ever more effort. Slovenia has already participated in these European Union operations very early in the Mare Nostrum and Sophia operations in the Mediterranean. We will continue to work in all our activities to establish and ensure a secure global environment and to effectively address the most crisis and acute focal points. A holistic approach to the European neighbourhood, which has turned into one of the world's most unstable areas in recent years, will continue to be an important aspect of the European Union's foreign policy activities, as well as of Slovenia. In doing so, it will be essential to pay particular attention to the future of the European Union's relations with the Russian Federation, both in the context of the resolution of the Ukrainian crisis and in the context of cooperation in action in the Middle East crisis areas. The wider view of the European Union must not simply respond to changes and current threats in the world. It must be more proactive and consolidate its role as co-creator of a secure and stable international environment. We want a strong European Union common foreign and security policy that ensures partnership with other global actors and works towards promoting stability, respect for the rule of law, human rights and sustainable development in the world. We will therefore continue to participate actively in the preparation of the European Union's global strategy, which will be presented by the High Representative for Foreign Affairs and Security Policy of the European Union to the European Council in June. In its consideration of current developments, we cannot go beyond the issue of terrorism, a series of terrorist attacks in recent months have shown the ability of terrorists to prepare, organise and implement violence against civilians in the region and also in the European Union. We can say that, in the EU, in the fight against terrorism, by adopting several important measures from words, we have moved on to very concrete actions. Among these is the new platform for combating terrorism and radicalism in the Western Balkans, which is a particularly sensitive region in Europe. The platform was created on the basis of a Slovenian initiative and we intend to continue to be very active here. Your Honor! In these very difficult times, we need a strong and undivided Europe. At the last European Council, we were able to agree on a solution to the British requirements for reform of the European Union. This is a very important signal that in difficult circumstances the Union is also able to act unitedly and find common solutions to very difficult strategic issues. The compromise agreement does not jeopardise the fundamental values and principles of the European Union, nor does it jeopardise the further development of the Economic and Monetary Union. It also takes into account Slovenia's concerns about limiting the free movement of workers and compliance with the basic Treaties of the European Union. The Slovenian interests are therefore ensured here. I believe that the membership of the United Kingdom in the European Union is in the mutual interest of the whole of the Union and its citizens, so I hope that the decision at the June referendum in the United Kingdom will be in favour of confirming membership in the European Union. I also believe that Europe must look forward together, focus on everything that connects us, and not on what separates us. This is the only way to remain competitive in the global world, to develop in line with common values and to respond effectively to many challenging common challenges. To date, any crisis in the European Union has been followed by a smaller or greater qualitative leap. I am also striving to see the current challenges followed by further progress in deepening integration. There must be no doubt here, Slovenia will be part of these processes. Therefore, we will also continue to advocate further deepening the economic and monetary union as a guarantee of greater stability in the euro area. This is certainly the most effective instrument to prevent new crises in this area. Dear Members and Members! Unfortunately, the debate on the present, which we live as a country and as a member of the European Union, is dominated by topics that are urgent sometimes, even if I have a somewhat graphic expression, by fire-fighting responses, but at the same time, we are still working a little less under the headlamps actively in areas that were not long before being the main core of European integration measures. In the Government's proposal for declarations and priorities, these things are also consciously recorded and will be taken into account in our further work for sure. Let me just touch them briefly in this presentation. Among the most visible achievements of the European Union and the symbols of European integration, where much further work is needed, is a single internal market where the free movement of persons, goods, services and capital is enjoyed by more than 500 million consumers and 20 million small and medium-sized enterprises. For Slovenia as a small and open economy, functioning in this internal market is essential as our companies create almost three quarters of their exports on the markets of the Member States of the European Union, so we will continue to seek to strengthen and deepen the internal market, including digital and to facilitate cross-border operations. This certainly has a positive impact on the competitiveness of the Slovenian economy and thus on faster economic development and the creation of new jobs. External trade is one of the key tools for promoting economic growth and employment. The next year will be a turning point in this area in a number of ways, as the European Union will enter into the final phase of negotiations on some important but politically sensitive agreements. In the negotiations, we explicitly advocate both the preservation of the right to regulation in the public interest and the preservation of the level of environmental, social, labour, health and other standards of the European Union and Slovenia. In particular, we are inflexible in the areas of agriculture and safe food. We are also firmly in favour of our views on the settlement of disputes between the State and the investor. Negotiations are of great interest to the public and therefore as much information as possible is needed in the course of these negotiations. With better information and an exchange of views, we face both the economy and civil society. Of course, the State Assembly is also informed of the state of negotiations. Slovenia is a country that lies at the crossroads of important transport links. The port of Koper has an unequivocal role not only for Slovenia but also for Europe as a whole, in particular for Central European countries. The railway connection between Koper–Divič is of paramount importance in this respect, due to the integration of the Slovenian and European economies with overseas countries mainly with Asia. That is why we want funding for the construction of the second track also to be provided through the European Fund for Strategic Investments of the so-called Juncker Plan. Slovenia also has a strong link with the Western Balkans and wants to strengthen the 10th Pan-European Corridor, so we have taken the initiative to establish an Alpine-West Balkan Corridor with the co-signatures Austria, Croatia, Serbia and Bulgaria. The solid and European energy union in relation to climate policy is not only in the interest of the European Union if I now touch on this issue. Slovenian consumers are certainly in favour of being able to choose the most cost-effective energy suppliers due to open competition. Even better, it will be ensured that gas supply is secure so that we are not at risk of being exposed to individual European market suppliers. We need to be aware that the cheapest energy is the one we don't consume. It is therefore essential to promote energy efficiency and, of course, to switch to renewable energy sources. In the field of climate change, the adoption of the Paris Agreement is a major step forward. High political ambition must be maintained and appropriate time and content activities must be ensured to preserve the European Union's leading role in this area. There are therefore two important tasks before the Union and Slovenia in the next period. In the United Nations, Member States and the European Union will sign the Paris Agreement in April this year, which will then need to be ratified as soon as possible, while at the same time the European Union will start looking for internal measures to achieve 40% reduction in greenhouse gas emissions by 2030. It is important for Slovenia that the Paris Agreement enters into force as soon as possible and that the European Union adopts such measures as will also take into account our specific transport needs. For the European Union and the Member States, an important process of transition to a circular economy is also starting, which will allow new business opportunities and jobs, taking into account the sustainable use of resources. Slovenia took an important step by adopting the Framework Programme for the Transition to the Green Economy adopted by the Government last October. We support the European Union's single political orientation towards the circular economy and sufficient flexibility in defining individual rules for its implementation at national level. Dear Members and Members! Allow me to conclude by thinking about what we must be aware of by all political actors and all branches of government, both within our national borders and within our wider community, of course, which we are talking about today. The citizens' trust in the European project or in state institutions will not be established and guaranteed by the populism of some virtual democracy on the street that rejects all others and others. This trust can only be assured by deliberate measures that will effectively offer an effective response to the concerns and uncertainties of citizens and help them to understand the positive meaning of our constitutional and European values and our common vision. Thank you.

ParlaMint-SI_2016-03-04-SDZ7-Redna-16.ana.u6	Thank you for your word, Mr. President. Dear Mr President of the Government, the Council of Ministers, distinguished colleagues and colleagues! At its 41st meeting, on 18 February 2016, the Foreign Policy Committee considered as a competent committee the proposal for a declaration of guidelines for the functioning of the Republic of Slovenia in the institutions of the European Union during the period from January 2016 to June 2017 submitted by the Government to the National Assembly pursuant to Article 110 of the Rules of Procedure of the National Assembly on 24 December last year. Representatives of the Ministry of Foreign Affairs, State Secretary Mrs Darja Bavdaž Kuret, Ministry of Defence and other representatives of the Government participated in the meeting of the Committee. The amendment to the proposal for a declaration was tabled by the Member of the Commission. Franc Trček, United Left Group. Further explanation of the proposal for a declaration was given by Darja Bavdaž Kuret, State Secretary at the Ministry of Foreign Affairs. In accordance with Article 5 of the Law on cooperation between the National Assembly and the Government in European Union matters, the Government reported at least once a year to the State Assembly on the situation in the Union and the situation of Slovenia in it. The National Assembly shall then adopt a position on the political guidelines for the functioning of the Republic of Slovenia in the institutions of the European Union in the next 18 months. She pointed out that the proposal for a declaration prepared by the Government for the past decade in a row contributes to the transparent and targeted functioning of the Republic of Slovenia in the institutions of the European Union and presents the dossiers to be considered on the bodies of the European Union in the future and sets out the guidelines to be advocated by the Slovenian representatives. The Declaration provides a framework for the preparation of the positions of the Republic of Slovenia, which the Government represents in its daily work in the institutions of the European Union. The debate raised the issue of the legal basis. There is a need for a change of law, some have thought, which governs the relationship between the National Assembly and the Government in matters of the European Union. In doing so, the Chairman of the Committee pointed out that the necessary amendments could already be included in the consideration of the amendment of the law in the first half of 2015. The inadequate timetable has been problematic, i.e. the timing of the consideration of the proposal for a declaration, as some opposition Members believe that the proposal for a declaration has been submitted too late since the second month of the year, and the National Assembly is only now accepting a declaration for 2016. The proposal for a declaration, also in the opinion of the opposition Members, does not address the current issues of the Union, does not reflect the priorities of the Declaration on the Foreign Policy of the Republic of Slovenia and the positions and priorities are too specific. Following the discussion, the Government identified a proposal for an amendment by a qualified proposer, Dr. Franz Trček. The amendment was not adopted in the vote. At the end of 9 votes in favour and without a vote, the Committee adopted the following opinion: "In the light of its competence, the Foreign Policy Committee considered the proposal for a declaration on guidelines for the functioning of the Republic of Slovenia in the institutions of the European Union during the period from January 2016 to June 2017 and adopted it in the text submitted." Thank you very much.

New Slovenia – Christian Democrats We are pleased that in this declaration we have written, of course, on the basis of the plebiscite decision of citizens and citizens of the Republic of Slovenia to join the European Union, that the European Union is our fundamental, fundamental political and valuable framework. That is why we Christian Democrats have always spoken, and this is how we have acted as a pro-European party. We are not Euro-Pianists because we simply do not see any other alternatives than the membership of the Republic of Slovenia in the European Union. We are therefore very careful about the views of the Republic of Slovenia in the various forums in the various institutions of the European Union. We are, of course, aware of the importance of the European Council. What role does the European Council play? The European Council shall set out the general policy orientation and priorities of the European Union. We know who's making it. It consists of Heads of State and Government of the European Union, President of the European Commission, High Representative for Foreign Affairs and Security Policy. The current President is Donald Tusk, who made a very important statement to us at yesterday's visit to Greece, and we also see here that the European Council and its President have actually been articulating some very clear guidelines concerning the current biggest problem that we are all dealing with within the EU and also Slovenia alone. This seems very important to us: "Do not believe smugglers, do not risk your lives and your money, everything is in vain!" End of quote. This may also be a closed session, which does not bother us, but it is even better that it is open, and ultimately the functioning of the institutions of the European Union is public. This is what we think, and we call for this reflection, and we want the government to think about it. Members of New Slovenia will support these guidelines, this declaration. It is virtually impossible to oppose this, but we agree with some who have also considered on the Committee on Foreign Policy and on the Committee on European Union Affairs that some structure or at least the time frame of this declaration should be changed. This declaration covers our guidelines for the next 18 months. At that time, the Council of the European Union will be chaired by three countries. The Netherlands now holds the presidency, which has already presented its priorities to us, the first is to remind us of a comprehensive approach to migration and international security, the second priority being the European Union, innovator and job creator, and so on. Well, Slovakia is the next country to preside over the Council of the European Union in the second half of this year. In fact, we do not yet know exactly what its priority is; it will certainly be very high among the priorities, if not again at the highest level, also the refugee and migrant crisis. As a third in this 18-month time interval, Malta will be chaired by the Council of the European Union. What the European Union will be like, what will touch all of us in the first half of next year, in fact, no one can predict today. I'm closing with one more thought. In some ways, it seems logical that even the Netherlands, President-in-Office of the Council of the European Union, has virtually paid no attention to the enlargement of the European Union. Yes, in a way we understand this, today we have significantly bigger problems, but the enlargement of the European Union is, in the view of New Slovenia, the most powerful political tool of the European Union. And we want this enlargement process to remain at least at working temperature, if I may say so. And in this sense, we also expect our representatives, representatives of the government in the institutions of the European Union, to strive to keep this enlargement process at working temperature, because we know the instability of the Balkans and it would be very good to encourage some Balkan countries to make European reforms, reforms that will lead to Euro-Atlantic integration. Thank you very much for your attention.

ParlaMint-SI_2016-03-04-SDZ7-Redna-16.ana.u10	Thank you so much for saying that, Mr. President. Dear Mr President of the Government, the Council of Ministers, colleagues and colleagues! Initially, today, at the beginning of 2016, the Declaration on Guidelines for the Functioning of the Republic of Slovenia in the Institutions of the European Union in the period January 2016-June 2017 can be particularly important both from a national perspective and because of the integrity of the functioning of European institutions, although it is only some framework for the successful integration of various Slovenian entities into the functioning of European institutions. It is essential that, after the adoption of the declaration of Slovenia, it is also possible to realize the intentions and commitments set out in the declaration. This may sound rhetorical, but let us ask ourselves to what extent the commitments and guidelines we adopted last year have been realized before the good year of adoption of the declaration. Above all, it is important to what extent the activity of our institutions has been established in the spirit of the declaration in the common institutions of the European Union. We probably do not have an accurate answer, but we can only assume that some of the tasks envisaged remain unfulfilled, but in my opinion the overall assessment for Slovenia is positive. Given the current challenges of the refugee migration crisis, it is understandable and necessary that the current declaration devotes much attention to this content. In this regard, our view is that Slovenia has clear messages that are at the same time sensitive to the refugee problem and clear to the protection of European values, including the consistent functioning of the Schengen system. I will focus in particular on national and national issues in the context of the text. The minority problem or issue of indigenous national communities falls within the competence of each member, and therefore there is little or no particular emphasis on this issue at EU level. Nevertheless, in our Group of Members, we believe that we should aim to make the basic requirements for the existence and development of the national community an important element of the operational functioning of the most important European institutions. While this initiative does not appear to be in the context of today's debate when we are talking about the mission of Slovenian entities to EU institutions, in fact, it can only be done with our recommendations in Brussels, Strasbourg and where we can accelerate this process to the point where it can be discussed honestly. Unfortunately, there has been no great readiness by the EU leadership in the past or by the leadership of the most influential countries. However, we believe that further Slovenian initiatives could also be important because of Slovenia's accession to this issue. The basic starting points set out in the first part of the declaration are more important today, as Mr President of the Government has already mentioned, than ever before. If we fail to maintain the EU in at least the form we have established over the past decades, they will be lost at all levels. Any negative changes, such as the Schengen regime, would particularly be felt by national communities and by many smaller nations, especially in our Central European and Eastern European spaces. The openness of the borders is a great achievement that generations have dreamed of in the previous historically turbulent century. For the values achieved, such as the rule of law, the security of citizens and property, financial stability, and so on, not to mention, we need to take particular action, and we therefore support the efforts made by Slovenia in particular to highlight and defend Slovenia in its activities. As I have already mentioned, in the declaration of content related to the so-called minority national communities, we hardly see what we are missing in our parliamentary group. There is talk of cultural and linguistic diversity, which is right. Although, in a given context, the focus is on diversity from the point of view of individual countries. Nevertheless, we have not decided on amendments because the text of the declaration is slightly more general in view of the specific circumstances mentioned than the previous ones. However, we cautioned - and this was particularly emphasized by the Commission's meeting for the National Communities of the National Assembly - that such an approach should not become a rule. We also point to the particular urgency of the term autonomy, which, unfortunately, has been questionable in the past for some members of the European Union and also in the field of expertise. We are convinced that such groups of national communities are part of the true European values, which are often important interconnections between neighbouring nations today and beyond. This applies to the communities that live in the Republic of Slovenia, as well as to Slovenians in place and also to other minorities, of course, in the European Area. I would like to point out that indigenousness or indigenousness does not mean any higher form in the classification of different nationalities or national communities or social groups, but merely a distinctive form, which differs in character and character from other forms of social groups such as economic migration, refugee groups and so on. All of these groups need different forms of treatment in terms of conservation and development or possible assistance. Autochthonousness is therefore no longer or less compared to other social groups, but it is one species and very characteristic of our geographical space. Also in communication with the most influential European institutions, we need to pay important and influential attention to this. I believe that autonomy is important not only from the point of view of minority national communities but also more and more for a number of smaller nations, so our initiative needs to be understood in a broader context than a European specificity or value. Finally, I should like to point out that in our Group we welcome the part of the declaration that highlights the importance of cultural heritage, because this is very important from the point of view of European extradition as well as from the national and local contexts. We also welcome Slovenia's takeover in this regard, but next year let it be dedicated to cultural heritage. A little outside the context of this, however, we must honestly admit that we have halved, unfortunately, the funds ourselves for cultural heritage over the past years, but we are now talking in general about this issue. Finally, let me say that we will support the proposed text of the declaration. Thank you very much.
ParlaMint-SI_2016-03-04-SDZ7-Redna-16.ana.u11	The word is given by the Group of the Party of the Modern Centre, for which Dr. Simona Kustec Lipicer.
ParlaMint-SI_2016-03-04-SDZ7-Redna-16.ana.u12	Thank you very much. Honourable President of the National Assembly, Honourable President of the Government, Honourable Council of Ministers, Honourable Colleagues and Colleagues! Today we are already considering the 10th proposal for a Declaration on guidelines for the functioning of the Republic of Slovenia in the EU institutions in a number of ways. Why is this document so important? Actually, for two reasons. On the one hand, because through it the Republic of Slovenia can and is also expected to set and highlight its political priorities within itself, i.e. its own national interests. On the other hand, it also builds and strengthens a single European action, i.e. the functioning of the European Union. In this sense, this declaration is primarily a document which could be referred to as a document through which the interests of the Republic of Slovenia are placed in the European environment. Before I focus a little more on today's consideration of the document, I would like to say one more thing. It is very important to be aware of the circumstances and the challenges and assessments of the current situation in which such documents arise. The European Union is currently facing an extremely big challenge. In the face of such a major challenge, this is linked to the fundamental issue of its future existence. Why? Because in several very important and central areas of its operation, it has fallen to the exam. This drop in the exam began years ago and is still being remedied in the context of financial and monetary economic activity. As a result, the development of the economic market, one of the main pillars and reasons for the establishment of the European Union, has also been very limited. What's going on now? The European Union is facing a crisis related to the fear of terrorism on the one hand and on the other with a fundamental value crisis, because it cannot find and is unable to find and find uniform solutions to address migration issues in this humanitarian context, i.e. in one of the fundamental aspects of its operation and its establishment. These are aspects related to values, to civilizational development. Here too is the European Union for testing. In connection with all of this, the Republic of Slovenia is also one of its Member States, whether wanted or not, indirectly and directly to the test. Therefore, this declaration is an extremely important and responsible document in the current situation, also from the point of view of the Republic of Slovenia. It is a question of how, through our own national interests and political priorities, we also communicate, we refer to the European Union with, I believe, a single and identical objective, further development, further consolidation of the European Union as a key political creation or link in this European space. We have already heard something about the interests themselves, political orientations and priorities in the declaration itself from the point of view of the Republic of Slovenia today. What is crucial now is that, in fact, the political orientations of Slovenia are very similar to those fundamental content and to the substantive orientations that the European Union also identifies in the context of its action, which in some ways reflects that we are well balanced, if we say so, with a single European policy and a vision of future development as a small but obviously very integrated and important Member State in this community. On the one hand, a large set and a wide range of these fundamental economic challenges and priorities, a solid economic monetary union, an EU development-oriented budget, including our national, of course, investment momentum, a solid energy union, advocacy and aspiration for the knowledge of the principles and implementation of the single market, transport policy, agriculture, the European Union's common trade policy, strengthening the European Union as an internationally important credible global actor. On the other hand, the awareness of the possibilities for the re-examination of the Member States to the point of departure, which we are now meeting in particular in the event of an agreement with the United Kingdom within the framework of the European Union. This agreement, which is being formulated, responds to the fact that the European Union is becoming itself often for its own purpose. Not only is it not able to address the current areas, the problems that should be addressed, including the institutions that it has set up for this purpose, do not work successfully, do not work effectively. This is reflected again in the second key set not only of our declaration, but of the whole of Europe's orientations at this moment – the targeting of migration policy, the fight against terrorism, and above all, the question or questioning of Europe's fundamental values today and in the future. Our proposal for a declaration seeks a consolidated functioning and a strong and strong European Union. This takes into account the special role, the special situation of the Republic of Slovenia. The Republic of Slovenia is through the content of this declaration, through what it reports, a strong, solid, very committed Member State of the European Union, but one which is also extremely wounded in this European Union because of all the current circumstances. That is why he needs and calls for help, for unity, for some kind of cooperation with this institution. We are the smallest country on the external Schengen border, we are a very important regional actor within the European Union, especially in the Western Balkans area, that is, in that part also a territory to which the European Union itself should spread in the future if it persists and if this proves to be of interest. And through all this, we report that we want to continue to remain a constructive Member State, which will, and which respects the rule of law of the European Union, advocates the development of the fundamental policies of the European Union, and, above all, to strengthen its values, values for which, when we join the European Union, we believe that our values are, too. The values of solidarity that contribute and contribute to stability, security and thus to the economic and social strength of the European Union and our countries. A stable, secure, united and economically successful European Union is also a fundamental framework for the development of our country. For this reason, Slovenia must remain at the centre of European integration in our opinion, but this European integration must also be seen. At today's meeting, we would like to see a broad political consensus reached by the Declaration on guidelines for the functioning of the Republic of Slovenia in the institutions of the European Union for the period January 2016-June 2017. To that end, we guarantee that the Group of the Parties of the Modern Centre will also contribute its votes. Thank you so much for saying that.

ParlaMint-SI_2016-03-04-SDZ7-Redna-16.ana.u18	Respectable both presidents, ministerial assembly, colleagues and colleagues! The Declaration defines the key tasks and guidelines that the Republic of Slovenia will follow in its operation in the institutions of the European Union. The document provides guidance in specific areas and is a broader framework for drawing up the views of the Republic of Slovenia, which the Government represents in its daily work in the institutions of the European Union. The Republic of Slovenia must strengthen its position in the European space and actively defend and enforce its views in key areas. Over the last few years, the European Union has been facing great trials. After the severe economic crisis that has shaken the European economy, it is possible to imagine that the greatest trials of Europe and the rest of the world are just ahead. We need to find ways to strengthen Europe to cope with the increasing challenges of modern times. We must be aware that we can solve these trials and only manage them together at European level with European solutions. Europe has been dealing too much with the consequences of the migrant crisis, rather than solving them at their source and addressing the causes. Even Europe seems to be still being hunted and searched for its own identity, so it is all the more important for Europe to draw up guidelines for a new migrant policy. Member States' behaviour must be primarily solidarity and focus on respect for the current legal order, the commitments already made and the protection of the external Schengen borders. Rapid action and effective cooperation with Member States and rapid action, in particular with partner third countries, are needed. Only in this way can we successfully manage the movement of people and address the root causes of migration pressures. The reconstruction of the asylum system, which will fairly divide the responsibility of the countries, is essential and will preserve solidarity. We need to set common European rules for asylum seekers to determine who is entitled to asylum in the countries of the European Union and to introduce clear rules on the separation of refugees and other migrants. At the same time, efforts must be made to strengthen the common foreign security policy aimed at halting violence and poverty in the restless parts of the world, and to increase the activity of the European Union's high foreign political representative. Only in this way can Europe successfully tackle the dimension of the migrant crisis and may even improve the impact of the response. The importance of economic growth and employment is particularly stressed by the Social Democrats. It is necessary to ensure a stable environment in which Slovenian and European companies will operate better in the future. We support the establishment of a Capital Markets Union where different levels of development, the size of capital markets in the Member States, are to be taken into account, which is to contribute to diversity and better supply of funding for businesses. The key objective must be to create and maintain quality jobs, so we must accelerate economic recovery, increase the competitiveness and innovation of our economy and smartly ensure the realization of the development potentials of both Slovenia and the European Union. It is essential to increase the employment opportunities of young people. This is important; young people need more support in the European space. We strongly welcome the adoption of all programmes and strategies aimed at improving the situation of young people. We will closely monitor, at both national and European level, the discussions on the adoption of free trade agreements, and in doing so we will oppose all solutions that would in any way undermine the social and economic situation of our citizens. As far as European funds are concerned, Slovenia is relatively successful. It should be noted that the administrative framework for drawing on European funds needs to be simplified as much as possible. The Ministry of Labour, Family, Social Affairs and Equal Opportunities, which was the most successful in drawing on European funds in the previous financial perspective, must be highlighted here. With the available 404 million, the Ministry has drained a good 402 million. The Ministry of Agriculture has also been very successful in drawing. Our farmers have successfully taken advantage of the possibility of applying for invitations to tender. Particular emphasis should be placed on the highest possible degree of self-sustainment and production of quality safe and price-competitive food. Data on agricultural production in Slovenia are positive, production increased from 4% to 6% last year and production value due to a drop in agricultural prices by about a percentage. It is important to promote the quality of Slovenian food against genetically modified organisms, raise consumers' awareness of healthy food and priority procurement of locally produced food. Emphasis should also be given to reducing food discards and releasing administrative burdens for farmers. We support the Strategic Framework for the Energy Union, which establishes a transition to a low-carbon, reliable and competitive economy. We must not take our planet for granted. The impacts of climate change are increasing. More frequent temperature fluctuations, floods, droughts and other weather extremes have been expected in the future, which have started to appear more frequently and intensively in the last decade and have caused enormous damage. We are still delaying measures and risking further changes, although we do not know how ecosystems and thus agriculture and people will react. The long-term global reduction in greenhouse gas emissions must be a priority for the European Union. We think that the guidelines for the functioning of Slovenia in European institutions are good, we must be aware of our belongings to Europe, and Slovenia is Europe. However, it is crucial that Slovenia in the European area, with innovation, dialogue and current themes, exercises its national interests as much as possible, while contributing to the thorough strengthening of the European Union. Social Democrats will be supported by the Proposal for a Declaration on Guidelines for the Functioning of the Republic of Slovenia in the Institutions of the European Union for the period January 2016-June 2017. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-SI_2016-03-04-SDZ7-Redna-16.ana.u52	Madam President, thank you for the floor. Dear colleagues and colleagues! In the Slovenian Democratic Party's Parliamentary Group, we have again decided to table a proposal for a law on amendments and additions to the Bank of Slovenia Act. We are convinced that such a step after amending and supplementing this law is urgently needed for a number of reasons. The fact is that the Slovenian banking system is not stable, that Slovenian taxpayers in the relatively short history of the independent state of Slovenia have been rehabilitated for the second time; last time in 2013 with more than EUR 5 billion. Given the situation and events in the Slovenian banking system in the Slovenian Democratic Party, we do not rule out the possibility that the need to rehabilitate the banking system by taxpayers will also be necessary in the third. In order to prevent this issue, we have probably tried for the fourth or fifth time to amend and supplement the Act amending the Bank of Slovenia Act, in particular on certain key issues. According to our assessment, this is the reporting of Banka Slovenije to the National Assembly. Undoubtedly, Banka Slovenije also informs the general public and the National Assembly with an annual and semi-annual report. In the Slovenian Democratic Party, we are convinced that such reporting is not appropriate and that the National Assembly should also have more information than is available to the general public. The law proposes an amendment that the Bank of Slovenia should also inform the National Assembly about confidential information in its reports. The second part of the amendment and additions to the Bank of Slovenia Act is mainly aimed at changing the composition of the Council of the Bank of Slovenia. We believe that Banka Slovenije is one of the only central banks in the field of the European Union where we have some kind of hybrid management system; or, in other words, that the administration is also its supervisor. We are confident that this change would introduce better and better control over the operation of the Bank of Slovenia. Today, for example, the Council of the Bank consists of the so-called 'bank of Slovenia'; in the morning, the Governors and Vice-Governors prepare their proposals, and in the afternoon, they re-sit and confirm these proposals. We believe in the Slovenian Democratic Party that such a system is inadequate. In the Slovenian Democratic Party, we estimate that supervision should also be supported by the Court of Auditors. It is unacceptable that the management of the Bank of Slovenia somehow does not let the Court of Auditors control the banking system itself. The fact is that the European Central Bank has its own supervisory system and that it is the European Court of Auditors. The fact is that the European Central Bank has repeatedly reported with various reports to the general public and to the professional public, regardless of the fact that this legislation requires it to do so once a year. The fact is also that we are convinced in the Slovenian Democratic Party that Banka Slovenije's leadership should be part of the system of public servants. This is about the officers elected by the National Assembly. As this is the first consideration of this law, we expect the support of the parliamentary groups in the Slovenian Democratic Party. We also expect support from the parliamentary groups for one reason: the last two vice-governors were elected by a large majority. Both vice-governors wrote in their programme that Banka Slovenije needs more, better control, change of legislation. For this reason we also expect support for the changes and additions to this Act on Banka Slovenije in the first reading. Thank you.
ParlaMint-SI_2016-03-04-SDZ7-Redna-16.ana.u53	Thank you very much. For an introductory explanation of the opinion, I give the floor to the representative of the Government, State Secretary at the Ministry of Finance, Mr Method Dragonji.
ParlaMint-SI_2016-03-04-SDZ7-Redna-16.ana.u54	Thank you very much, Madam Vice President. Dear Members and Members! The Government of the Republic of Slovenia considers as crucial that the proposed amendments and additions to the Bank of Slovenia Act affect the independence of the Bank of Slovenia, as set out in the Treaty on the Functioning of the European Union and the Statute of the European System of Central Banks and of the European Central Bank. In determining the relationship between Banka Slovenije and the National Assembly, it is necessary to draw from the Constitution of the Republic of Slovenia, which gives importance to the independence of the Bank of Slovenia and the decisions of the Bank of Slovenia, as it provides that the central bank is independent and directly accountable to the National Assembly. However, the concept of responsibility to the National Assembly cannot be compared with the concept of control over the Bank of Slovenia, which would, in particular, be inconsistent with its institutional independence, which prohibits external interference in its decisions. The proposer proposes to supplement Article 26 of the Bank of Slovenia Act regulating the reporting of Banka Slovenije to the National Assembly and provides that the report of Banka Slovenije should be comprehensive and, if necessary, should also include confidential information and information. The Government considers that the reporting of the Bank of Slovenia to the National Assembly is appropriate, since, in addition to the information covered by the annual report of the Bank of Slovenia, it sends it other information and information on request, so I do not think that any change in this area is necessary. With regard to the information provided by the representatives of the National Assembly on the work of the Bank of Slovenia, the Government estimates that representatives of the National Assembly have the opportunity to obtain information from the Bank of Slovenia since, in accordance with the applicable Bank of Slovenia law, a representative of the National Assembly committee responsible for finance and monetary policy may participate in the meetings of the Board of the Bank of Slovenia. This competence of the committee in terms of obtaining information is actually stronger than the regular reporting to the National Assembly, as proposed by the drafters of the amendment of the law. The draft law provides for a supplement which provides that the coordination of the status of members of the Council of Banka Slovenije with the provisions of the Banka Slovenije Act and the conditions for the performance of the function shall be determined by the Commission to prevent corruption, which shall also establish the incompatibilities of the performance of the function of a member of the Council. In this case, too, the principle of the independence of the Bank of Slovenia must be respected in accordance with the European statutory framework of the European Central Bank. The opinion of the European Central Bank, dated 18 September 2008, was forwarded to the National Assembly. 12. I will not repeat the findings or quote the individual findings of this opinion. In general, the opinion highlights the need to ensure the independence and independence of the central bank. With regard to the proposal for Banka Slovenije to draw up and implement a financial plan, a employment policy and has the means of payment in accordance with the laws and regulations laid down for direct users of the State budget, we believe that this would also constitute an interference with the independence of the Bank of Slovenia. The position of national central banks should enable them to have the resources themselves to meet the objectives of the European System of Central Banks in an appropriate and effective manner. In principle, the Government of the Republic of Slovenia does not oppose the proposal to provide the Court of Auditors with a legal basis for the revision of the Bank of Slovenia, but takes into account the requirements set out in this opinion by the European Central Bank. The scope of audit powers has been coordinated at European level for several years between the European Court of Auditors and the ECB and, once the relevant decisions have been taken at European level, we will transfer them to Slovenian legal arrangements. The Government of the Republic of Slovenia, for those reasons and on the basis of the opinion delivered by the ECB in this regard, does not support the proposal for a law. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-SI_2016-03-04-SDZ7-Redna-16.ana.u56	Madam President, thank you for your opportunity. Honourable Secretary of State, dear colleagues and colleagues! The proposed Law on Amendments and Supplements to the Bank of Slovenia Act, which we are considering today in the context of the general debate, certainly raises some important questions, for which it is right to find appropriate answers and solutions, since this law, the Bank of Slovenia Act, is the fundamental document for its operation. Acting on the basis of good legal solutions that further strengthen confidence as a national banking regulator in and outside Slovenia, as part of the Eurosystem is responsible for designing and implementing decisions of the Council of the European Central Bank. We are of the opinion that some of the proposed solutions are appropriate and it is therefore reasonable that we should also take account of these changes in the law. Let us see for ourselves the proposal and the solutions offered. Request for more comprehensive reports than Banka Slovenije does and publishes publicly. The proposer would like Banka Slovenije to submit reports to the National Assembly, which could also contain possible confidentiality and would require the closure of the meeting to deal with such reports. They also propose that the reports be considered at least half a year and not more once a year, such as practice. The applicants also wish that, in cases where the Bank of Slovenia identifies the need for State aid in which one of the banks is located, it shall immediately inform the National Assembly, of course, by stating the reasons, the role and responsibility of the Bank of Slovenia’s management, the Custodian, in relation to State aid and Bank of Slovenia’s control measures. Furthermore, the composition of the Council of Banka Slovenije would remain the same as before, the governor and four vice-governors, but the vice-governors would have a maximum of 20% employment at Banka Slovenije. Participation in Council of the Bank of Slovenia meetings. Today, in addition to the members of the Council, the president of the Committee on Finance and Monetary Policy and the Minister of Finance are also invited to participate, as is said the opposition. This associate member of the Council would also be without a vote. The following, discharge and reasons for the dismissal of the members of the Council of the Bank of Slovenia. It is proposed to amend the procedure and the reasons for the discharge. This would be decided by the National Assembly as new reasons for violating the laws and constitution and for calling on the Court of Auditors to be dissolved. At least 15 Members would be given the initiative to resolve, and they should attach a proposal for evidence of a violation or an audit report. A member of the Council whose resolution is proposed could respond in writing or orally to any reported violations. The members of the Committee would decide by a majority of the members of the Committee on Finance who would be the competent working body. The National Assembly would approve the discharge by a majority of the votes of all Members. Next, the Governor of Banka Slovenije could relieve the director or deputy director of the department for no reason. Next. The Bank of Slovenia should prepare and implement financial plans, employment policy and forgiveness and have the means of payment in accordance with the laws and regulations applicable to direct and direct budgetary users. Employees would become public servants subject to the public sector wage system. Next, the proposal also limits the number of mandates to the Governor and other members of the Council of the Bank of Slovenia. Finally, the proposal also provides that the Court of Auditors shall revise the correctness and prudence of the Bank of Slovenia and its supervisory activities. The law also allows the disclosure of confidential bank data to the Court of Auditors. I must certainly stress here that most of the proposed solutions are such that they are likeable, some also respond satisfactorily to the current questions, actions and supervision of the Bank of Slovenia. In all of this, we are obliged to take into account that the Bank of Slovenia and its members are independent in the performance of their duties and are not bound by the Decisions, Positions and Directions of the State or of any authority, nor may they be addressed in accordance with instructions or guidance. From 1 January 2007, the Bank of Slovenia is also a member of the Eurosystem, in accordance with the Treaty establishing the European Community and the Statute of the European System of Central Banks and of the European Central Bank, which must also fully implement in its operation. In the Group of Non-Aligned Members, we believe that some of the points of the bill can be supported. This, the Court of Auditors' audit, is why we have given the mandate of the Court of Auditors more than once. The implementation of the same principles as are applicable to the public sector: financial and human resources plans, salary scale, anti-corruption clause, participation of representatives of the opposition at the Council of the Bank of Slovenia – we do not really see any reason why this would not be the case – the information of the National Assembly in the context of the supervision of the Bank of Slovenia's work. The bill also contains solutions that we cannot support: the way and reasons for the discharge of the governor or vice-governor. It follows from the proposed text that 15 members of the National Assembly could propose a discharge by providing evidence of a violation of the law and the Constitution. It's not entirely clear what that means. In Slovenia it is only the courts that say they are in violation of the law and the Constitutional Court is the one that says they are in violation of the Constitution, otherwise the sentence of innocence applies. In addition, the independence of the central bank, as defined in the European legal order, must be highlighted here. The Statute of the European System of Central Banks provides that the governors may be dismissed only if they no longer fulfil the conditions or if they have committed a serious breach, with the possibility of bringing the matter before the EU court. Next, the number of possible mandates. In view of the need to repeat the whole procedure from the proposal to the appointment itself at each appointment, we believe that the MPs of the National Assembly have already been given the opportunity not to appoint the Governor or Vice-Governor again, who has not been successful in his work so far as the Governor or Vice-Governor has been. However, a successful person may continue to work, irrespective of the number of mandates. In the Group of Non-Aligned Members, we believe that the proposal for a law is suitable for further consideration, as unacceptable amendments to the law can also be eliminated in further adoption procedures with amendments. Thank you for your attention.

Of course, we support that the Commission of Inquiry obtains all the necessary material from banks that have been recapitalised. We also cannot agree that the proposed Law on Amendments and Supplements to the Bank of Slovenia Act of 21. Ten. 2015 intervenes in several areas of law that have specific regulatory arrangements and this is reflected in the last articles. Such amendments to individual laws in different areas under one law, which at the same time combines specific temporary legal arrangements in particular areas, have a significant impact on the opacity, uncertainty, inconsistentity of the legal system. The clarity, transparency, certainty and consistency of the legal regime and the legal system are, in general, fundamental elements of the constitutional principle of the rule of law, as enshrined in Article 2 of the Constitution of the Republic of Slovenia. The parliamentary group of the Modern Centre Party will not support and vote against the proposal on the draft Law on Amendments and Supplements to the Bank of Slovenia Act at first reading. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-SI_2016-03-04-SDZ7-Redna-16.ana.u64	Thank you for your word and a nice greeting to all on behalf of the Group of Social Democrats. When discussing the proposal for a law on amendments and additions to the Bank of Slovenia Act, we would like to point out immediately in the introduction that democratic control of independent central banks is a fundamental part of people's trust in the banking system and this trust has been strongly conceived in the past decade for the reason that global, large capital, which has also been laying down its rules since the start of the crisis, has also become a state debt, but has also very clearly pointed to governments and has imposed this responsibility to resolve the financial crisis on governments and private debt. This is the point where people's trust has certainly been extremely conceived, because the burden that has fallen on people is shown in the form of a public debt, which is still one of those elements that, of course, does not release either investment to a large extent or the wages themselves, which are the basic and integral part of the existence of people, and, finally, many times is also an intervention in the health system, the school system and, of course, also in the pension system. We also believe that the European Central Bank cannot oppose democratic control being carried out in confidence and through the most competent independent supervisory authority such as the Court of Auditors. Banka Slovenija is responsible to the National Assembly after the stop, and we are therefore the only ones called to supervise, but without the information of this supervision you cannot effectively and successfully implement, because it is also an extremely difficult and, of course, specific area of expertise. The thoughts we have on this amendment of the law are, to the greatest extent, about whether this system will then focus primarily on trying to interfere with the independence of both the Governor of Banka Slovenije and, of course, the members of the Council of Banka Slovenije. Unfortunately, many times in Slovenia it happens that measures and thinking about names are concentrated, not on content. This has often caused us great problems, since there have been many cases where we have come up with a very serious problem in individual institutions due to ineffective action. Given that Banka Slovenije is the highest regulatory authority in the field of banking, there is an urgent need for caution and, above all, coordination between all actors, which can help us to adopt quality solutions. We believe that this balance between all stakeholders, both between the opposition and the coalition, the Court of Auditors and the Bank of Slovenia, should be achieved as soon as possible. In particular, because the last case of the appointment of the Fiscal Council in law, which, of course, we want to exercise effective control over public spending through financial instruments, which we have also incorporated into the constitution, shows that the Fiscal Council has not actually been appointed, because it has not been coordinated with the opposition, and this is the great disadvantage that has come to the National Assembly, knowing that a whole two-thirds majority must, of course, be obtained. ParlaMint-SI_2016-03-04-SDZ7-Redna-16.ana.u66	Thank you, Vice President. Good-bye, everybody! This thesis that the greatest crisis of capitalism since World War II could be avoided if we had better control of the central bank and if the Court of Auditors revised its business is, of course, ridiculous. It is clearly intended to divert attention from the real causes and those responsible for the crisis. The first factor was the global capitalist crisis that began in the United States. Directly as a result of the decline in Slovenian exports, our GDP fell by 8% in 2009. Since 2009, absolutely nothing has happened to protect us from a similar crisis. Other factors are the structural errors of the euro area. The crisis in Slovenia was longer and worse than in Eastern Europe, because we entered the euro area at the worst of times with cheap credit. Interestingly, right-wing people have been always stressing how the economic growth in Yugoslavia was dependent on credit, but they do not want to know anything about the 13 billion credits on which growth was dependent during the first government. However, today the euro area is as dysfunctional as it was before the crisis. Only now can I turn to the third factor and role of the Bank of Slovenia. The scope of the crisis is greater than inevitable, due to the wrong actions of the Janša government, which was working contrary to the agreement with the Bank of Slovenia. For a precise list, see the Bank of Slovenia report on the causes of the capital deficit of the banks in March 2015, only three will be mentioned. Firstly, it was the construction of motorways with Darsa's debt rather than from the budget; the second, the reduction of taxes on high incomes, the abolition of taxes on paid salaries and the reduction of corporate and third income tax, the privatisation of companies owned by Soda and Kad. These are all measures that have increased the amount of money in the economy that has already been overheated. The result was a major structural deficit in the country, the largest in 2008. It is no wonder, therefore, that even the public debt increased during the first Janša government. At the end of 2008, €8.2 billion was owed, i.e. 787 million more than in 2004. This brings us to the role of Banka Slovenije. The Bank of Slovenia should tighten credit conditions and capital requirements. My colleagues from NSI would say that this was not allowed because we are part of the ECB. However, in 2010, Banka Slovenije increased its credit requirements on its own at the wrong time. That's when the credit crunch started, which is still going on. However, I do not believe that the Court of Auditors' audit could prevent this. Perhaps only some Taiwanese loans to church financial holding companies Zvon One and Zvon Two, construction companies, Bavčar's Istrabenz and so on could be prevented. Prof. Prašnikar estimates that from 13 billion there were 1.7 billion, the rest went to basic activity and real estate. In short, about 15% of the loans could be due to poor supervision. Now that we are clear what this law will not achieve, we can assess what it can accomplish. We are in the United Left all the time in the position that the Court of Auditors is already competent to revise the operation of the Bank of Slovenia. The purpose of such an audit would be to investigate the measures taken to rehabilitate banks. Two weeks ago, an opinion of the European Advocate was published concerning the deletion of small shareholders in Slovenian banks. It follows from this opinion that there was no European legal basis for decommissioning. It also confirms that the valuation at the liquidation value, with which NLB’s overnight capital was reduced from plus 824 million to minus 1.5 billion, was unjustified. However, we know from earlier that the assumptions of this valuation were wrong, given that the difference between valuations by two methods was as much as 1.5 billion or 45 percentage points. In Spain, where such reviews were carried out one year earlier, there was virtually no difference. I will not give any other suspicious details, the balance sheet is clear – we have banks that have too much capital and will now be privatised at a price lower than book value. This should be the last alarm to stop privatisation in the banking sector. This is certainly a school case of a problem for which the Court of Auditors exists. And if the Esdees law is to be supported, so that the Court of Auditors hopes to investigate this, of course we will support it. If I understand the Government's position correctly, the Government also supports certain powers of the Court of Auditors. Finally, I need to identify up to some new solutions that have not yet been identified in last year's bill. We support the proposal to set wages in Banka Slovenije as well as in the other public sector, including the Governor and Vice-Governors. We also support the proposal that the Bank of Slovenia should draw up a financial and human resources plan, like all other bodies. We support this because Banka Slovenije cannot be a country in the country. Every public authority in the last instance is responsible to citizens and must justify the purposeful use of funds. This is not an intervention in autonomy or if someone demands such autonomy for Banka Slovenije, we reject it. If I leave behind the objectives and ideological assumptions of this proposal, the balance of good and bad solutions is certainly positive. ParlaMint-SI_2016-03-04-SDZ7-Redna-16.ana.u74	Thank you very much, Chairman, for your word. Certainly today's topic we are discussing, the proposal for a law on amendments and additions to the Bank of Slovenia Act is extremely important and I support it. First, I might consider Banka Slovenije as an institution. The Bank of Slovenia is the central Bank of the Republic of Slovenia and is a legal person of public law established by law in 1991 and is independent and accountable to the National Assembly. This is stated in Article 152 of the Constitution. The Bank of Slovenia is truly independent and independent in the performance of its tasks, it is said that its functioning must not be affected by political or any other partial interests, and it is often emphasized, even today we have heard several times, precisely this independence of the central bank, but as an institution, as I have already said, is also responsible to citizens and their elected representatives, Parliament says. It is therefore right that we are here today to discuss how the Central Bank, our Bank of Slovenia, is responsible to us. In this definition of the relationship between Banka Slovenije and the National Assembly, as I have already said, it is necessary to proceed from the Constitution of the Republic of Slovenia, which already gives meaning to the independence of the Bank of Slovenia, and this responsibility is based on the arrangement that the National Assembly is also the one who appoints the Governor and the Vice-Governor. The question is how to further explain the concept of responsibility and whether to compare it with the concept of control over the Bank of Slovenia, which, in particular, could be incompatible with its institutional independence, which prohibits any external interference in its decisions. Thus, this institutional independence of the central bank, which is prescribed by the legal order, means that the Bank of Slovenia is not legally bound by the decisions of the positions and instructions of the state or any authority, nor should they address them in accordance with instructions or guidelines. And here, in principle, if this is signed by law, with the Statute of the European System of Central Banks and the European Central Bank, I don't know what it is that, alone as President of the Committee on Finance and Minister, which we are in some of the roles that we are policy, we can work at Council of the Bank of Slovenia meetings if this is expressly prohibited by the Statute. The very independence of the Bank of Slovenia can be discussed not only in the light of our Parliament, but also of the entire European system. This means how independent and independent Banka Slovenije is today. As from 1 January 2007, it is in line with the Treaty establishing the European Community and the Statute of the European System of Central Banks and of the European Central Bank, and the Bank of Slovenia is also a member of the Eurosystem, which forms and implements the decisions of the Council of the European Central Bank. Thus, the Bank of Slovenia is a member of the Eurosystem, carrying out its mission and tasks within the framework of the Council of the European Central Bank, composed of all the governors of the national central banks of the euro area and members of the Executive Board of the European Central Bank. The Bank of Slovenia also acts as a member of the European System of Central Banks, composed of the European Central Bank and the national central banks of all Member States of the European Community. Representatives of the Bank of Slovenia are also members of the European Central Bank. The basic function of the committees of the European Central Bank and the fundamental function of these committees is to advise the Council of the European Central Bank. Banka Slovenije also participates in the European Systemic Risk Committee, which was established in 2010. This committee is part of the European financial surveillance system and is intended to monitor the EU financial system. All financial market supervisors are also a member of the Board, as is the Governor of Banka Slovenije. With the establishment of the European Systemic Risk Committee and the adoption of laws on macroprudential supervision of the financial system at the end of 2013, Banka Slovenije also assumed the role of the Secretariat of the Financial Stability Committee of Slovenia, composed of representatives of Banka Slovenije, AZN and ATVP and the Ministry of Finance. With effect from 15 October 2014, the Council Regulation of the EU transferred the specific tasks of the credit institution prudential control policy to the European Central Bank, which took over the supervisory powers in November 2014, a year after the entry into force of the Regulation. The competent supervisory authority of Slovenia is Banka Slovenije. This transferred part of the supervisory mandate of the Systemic Important Banks to this Single Supervisory Mechanism and, of course, required the intensive and serious involvement of the Bank of Slovenia. For all this reason, the Bank of Slovenia must both respect the provisions of the Treaty establishing the EU and the Treaty on the Functioning of the European Community and the Statute of the European System of Central Banks and of the European Central Bank and the Bank of Slovenia Act in relations with national and European authorities. These contracts and statutes must be fully implemented by Banka Slovenije in its operation. And I am now, in the opinion of the European Central Bank, on the proposal of this law, proposed by the proposer. Before any amendment to national legislation, national authorities, including the legislator, are obliged to consult the European Central Bank on draft legislation falling within the scope of the competence of the European Central Bank. This duty stems from the Article of the Treaty establishing the European Community and its non-compliance could lead to proceedings for breach of the Treaty before the Court of Justice of the European Community. A national regulation which would be adopted in contravention of the ECB's opinion or which would have been adopted in breach of its procedural requirements would have been inapplicable. This is also illustrated by the case law. And the ECB's view on the adoption of this regulation, in a few points, points out certain solutions that are not in line with the legal order and are proposed by this law, or that it is referring to what Mr Logar has already said that these provisions should be supplemented, implemented or amended. In this light, it is also necessary to assess the appropriateness of the individual solutions proposed by the proposer. As legislator, we must be aware that the legal solutions should be in line with the requirements laid down by the Treaty establishing the EU, the Statute of the European System of Central Banks and the European Central Bank, as well as the guidance set out in its opinions by the European Central Bank. If I touch on the ECB's opinion in this point, this opinion on the amendment of the Bank of Slovenia Act focuses on this issue, according to my assessment; it is said that disclosure of confidential data relating both to data from Banka Slovenije's control carried out and to statistical information from Banka Slovenije. The ECB stresses that the possible exchange of such information and information should be in accordance with EU rules governing such exchange and the protection of business secrets. The European Central Bank also stresses in several places that confidential information can only be exchanged if it is necessary to carry out the tasks of the authority receiving that information. It also points out that, with regard to confidential supervisory information, EU law provides for a framework within which such information can be exchanged. In this regard, the European Central Bank notes that solutions in the proposed amendment, which provide for an exception to such requirements for the protection of confidential information, raise concerns from the point of view of a number of provisions of EU law. While the European Central Bank explains that, on the basis of a directive governing the area concerned, it is possible to disclose certain information relating to the prudential supervision of institutions, namely the authorities with investigative powers, but such disclosures also need to take into account certain restrictions, such as the clearly defined mandate of the authority receiving such information, and the condition that the information required must be strictly necessary to fulfil such a mandate. Consequently, the ECB notes that EU rules relating to the exchange of information and business secrets do not provide a legal basis for disclosing confidential information to the National Assembly as such. This is how I understand this, so that this article should probably be harmonised and supplemented, as stated in the ECB's opinion.
