However, it is necessary to provide, on a legal basis, four protections specifically addressed or identified. Thus, the scope of control should be clearly defined within the legal framework, in the activities of the independent external auditors of the national central bank should not be prejudiced, in accordance with the principle of institutional independence as set out in the Treaty and the Statutes, such an audit should respect the prohibition of giving instructions to national central banks and its decision-making bodies and should not prejudice the tasks of national central banks related to the European System of Central Banks and should be implemented on a non-political, independent and purely professional basis. Thus, the Court of Auditors, as I understand the ECB's opinion, can take these four points into account, saying that these safeguards can ensure the independence of the Bank of Slovenia. We are moving on to the 6th item of the agenda, this is to consider the proposal for a law on ratification of amendments to the Agreement on the Conservation of Cetaceans and Dolphins of the Black Sea, the Mediterranean Sea and the Atlantic Area. The draft law was tabled by the Government. In order to further explain the proposal, I give the floor to the representative of the Government, the State Secretary at the Ministry of Environment and the premises of Mrs Lidija Stebernak.
ParlaMint-SI_2016-03-04-SDZ7-Redna-16.ana.u137	Dear, President, honourable Members and Members, distinguished representatives of the Government! The proposal for this law is a minor amendment to the basic agreement ratified by Slovenia in 2006. The purpose of the basic agreement on the feeding of whales and dolphins of the Black Sea, the Mediterranean Sea and the Atlantic area is to preserve these populations as an integral part of the marine ecosystem for the benefit of present and future generations. The treatment of migratory species living in a large area is therefore in the interest of the international community and concerted international action is necessary. The activities carried out under this Agreement help the Republic of Slovenia to preserve those types of dolphins that are constantly present in the waters of our jurisdiction and to fulfil those agreed obligations towards the European Union that also require the protection of these species. The amendment of the Agreement increases the area of the Agreement in the Atlantic from the smaller part of the waters under the jurisdiction of Spain and Portugal to the whole waters under their jurisdiction, without the Azores, Madeira and the Canary Islands. Scientific findings suggest that whale and dolphin living populations are more closely related to the Mediterranean than to the North Seas. The amendment to the Agreement has no additional financial and administrative implications for the Republic of Slovenia. The current membership is approximately EUR 2 000 per year. The ratification of this amendment to the agreement, which is very much pursued by the Principality of Monica, continues to place Slovenia in line with the confidence of the worthy Mediterranean countries and allows continued donor support to the Principality of Monaco and other donors by the Slovenian efforts to preserve the marine environment and protected areas. So far, these support has been given to professional institutions on our coast, the Morigenos and the Marine Biological Station in Piran and the Triglav National Park, for example, by a wider society with a structured infrastructure to view natural sights in Ankaran. Thank you.
ParlaMint-SI_2016-03-04-SDZ7-Redna-16.ana.u138	The draft law was treated by the Committee on Foreign Policy as a mother working body, which reported in writing to the assembly. We are moving on to a presentation on the position of the Joint Left Group. Dr. Matthew T. Vatovec has the word.
ParlaMint-SI_2016-03-04-SDZ7-Redna-16.ana.u139	Thank you, Mr. President. Good evening, everyone! The Agreement on the Conservation of Cetaceans and Dolphins of the Black Sea, the Mediterranean Sea and the Atlantic Area connects countries from the Mediterranean and Black Sea regions in joint efforts to preserve whales and dolphins in the Mediterranean and Black Sea and to improve knowledge of these animals. The amendment of the agreement extends the areas of the agreement with the current smaller part of the waters under the jurisdiction of Spain and Portugal to all waters in the Atlantic Ocean under their jurisdiction, without the Azores, Madeira and the Canary Islands, as we have heard. This will make it possible to achieve the objectives of this Agreement more successfully. That the agreement is still important, recent studies on rising underwater noise in areas of agreement hindering animal orientation, feeding, communication, increasing stress and potentially cumulative effects prove important. Noise flares are often covered with underwater habitats. The recent study on toxic compounds in the Mediterranean and the Black Sea also points to the relevance and relevance of the agreement. In the waters of these seas we still find compounds that have been banned for almost a century, and in addition a host of newer by-products of the dirty industry. The most pressing problem is the high levels of PCB compounds, which in marine mammals cause deterioration of the immune system and significantly reduced fertility. For example, the population of Orcs in the waters of Western Mediterranean is on the brink of extinction. The Agreement enables the identification and resolution of all these problems, which are still in place under the seabed of the Adriatic Sea and planned construction of gas terminals in the light of exploration of natural gas and oil. We welcome the regular updating of the provisions of the agreements that strengthen its commitments and the ability to achieve their objectives in the United Left and we will be happy to support ratification.

The Council of Justice submitted to the National Assembly a proposal for the election of Ms Mojce Dubravica to the judicial office of the District Court at the Labour Court in Maribor. In order to further explain the proposal, I give the floor to Mrs Ionica Marflak Trontel, Secretary of the Council of Justice.
ParlaMint-SI_2016-03-04-SDZ7-Redna-16.ana.u145	Mr President, Members and Members of Parliament! The Labour Court in Maribor is a specialised first-degree court competent for the trial of individual and collective labour disputes in Maribor, Murska Sobota, Ptuj and Slovenj Gradec. There are currently only 6 judges and the President of the Court in this court. In recent years, this court has left seven judges or judges, either for retirement or for election to the Supreme Labour and Social Court. Of the seven courts, only one was replaced until this invitation to tender. Due to the nature of the disputes that this court is dealing with and which are generally of existential importance to the parties, it is extremely important that the cases be dealt with promptly. In order to ensure the continued success of this court, it was necessary to refer the case to the court in question. In doing so, it should be pointed out that other working courts across the country do not have reserves, so it is not possible to redistribute cases or to relocate judges in this legal area. The invitation to tender included 13 candidates, of which 3 District Judges, with previous judicial experience in other legal areas, one lawyer and the other candidates are professional colleagues or co-workers in different courts. The specific selection process was carried out in two stages through the phase-out of candidates and using different methods to determine the knowledge and abilities of candidates, by means of a view of the documentary documentation and oral interviews. In the first stage of the selection procedure, the Court carried out an assessment of the suitability of candidates for the seat of the Court of Justice on the basis of written documentation, taking into account in particular the final opinion on the candidates applied by the President of the Labour Court in Maribor, the experience of the trial so far, and the average evaluation at the Faculty of Law and the legal examination. Subject to these criteria, he invited nine candidates to the oral interview. All three judges and lawyers, too. On the basis of all the documents and taking into account the oral interview and the opinion of the President of the Labour Court in Maribor, who was also present in the interview with the candidates, the Council of Justice assessed that Mojca Dubravica was a positive candidate. Mojca Dubravica, born in 1978, has been employed since 2006 in the Supreme Court of Maribor as a senior judicial adviser, where she has been working in the civil field all the time. He is currently working for the Office of the President of the Court in addition to his work in the civil field. The senior judges with whom she works assess her expertise and the ability to resolve legal issues as being above average and add that she has very good capacity to reason on decisions and verbal and written statements. They also pointed out that the average amount of work should be carried out, taking fully into account the expected deadlines for individual tasks and that its written proposals for decisions are both professional and highly reasoned or highly qualitative in terms of content and language. Her professional subordination, her creative thinking ability to solve more complex legal problems, and her very good analytical and logical thinking have also been convinced by the Court of Justice in an oral interview where she stood out positively in comparison with other candidates. Its answers to the professional questions raised, both civil, procedural and labour law, confirmed its extensive in-depth expertise from various legal areas, which is necessary in the case-law of the District Court in the Labour Court. The Council of Justice thus assessed that Mojca Dubravica of all candidates was the most suitable candidate for judicial service at the district court in the Labour Court in Maribor and therefore proposed her to the National Assembly for election to that court seat. Thank you very much.

ZMZ-1 introduces border procedures which will be accelerated, these are two facts, but this very general provision, as enshrined in the law, is directly contrary to the directive to which they refer. Article 31 of this Directive makes clear when Member States may determine the application of the accelerated procedure, which is the illegal entry into the Republic of Slovenia and the suspicion that it poses a threat to national security. Just as this border procedure, which is an accelerated procedure enshrined in the current law, it goes much wider and, of course, enables this accelerated procedure to be misused in some way. The Directive also provides that, in the case of border procedures, Member States may provide for the testing of decisions by official duty, which clearly indicates the gravity of the requirement for specific protection of fundamental guarantees in such procedures. And we have in this law, and this amendment alone, gives this – Mr President, please ensure peace in the Chamber – a basis for mass extermination [[…]]

We are now in a situation where, of course, this suspension applies only to one of those points, and at the first, second and fourth points it will still exist. In short, people who ask for asylum if such a decision is taken will simply be expelled from the country before they can complain. It is again another violation of what are international acts and also this directive itself. In short, I reiterate that we are implementing the directive in such a way that we are also violating it in several points. The same is true of the provision you are deleting, which is said to be a possible appeal to the Supreme Court, which is, of course, totally unacceptable, in this situation, the asylum applicant remains merely an exceptional legal remedy, which we call an audit, and is in fact a mechanism that is completely non-self-sufficient. It is not a matter of substantive decision-making but merely procedural, so it is even more problematic as far as it is concerned. Here it is in some ways incomprehensible why this insistence and this is also why this procedural complication has taken place at this meeting, because it is actually a law that is important, a law that decides on the lives of those who have decided to come to Europe, and in this way, I would say, restricting the debate and also such a way of sharpening is totally unacceptable, as far as this is concerned. In short, the amendment we are proposing is trying to provide at least this basic legal remedy, it is said, the possibility of appeal to the Supreme Court. We have also seen that most of the complaints that went to the Supreme Court were later settled; in many cases, there are violations in this procedure, and now this remedy for legal protection is terminated. The suspension is also contrary to international law, contrary to the directive and should remain in force at all four points in the law, but not only in one.
